EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

Environmental Group Australia Pty Ltd (EGA) was engaged by Mosman Council (hereafter referred to as ‘the client’), to undertake a Desktop Phase 1 Environmental Site Assessment of the property located within Lot 1 DP936479, Lot 1 DP936480 and Lot 6 DP1099253, 309 Military Road, Mosman NSW (hereafter referred to as ‘the site’) (refer to Figure 1 and Figure 2).

The client is preparing to lodge a planning proposal to implement the draft Mosman Masterplan, which proposes increased housing capacity across the study area, including amendments to the planning controls at the site to facilitate redevelopment.

Purpose and Objectives

A Desktop Phase 1 Environmental Site Assessment was required to assess the likelihood that the site had been contaminated by past or present activities and to form a preliminary view on the extent and degree of contamination. The objectives of the investigation were to:

• Provide advice on whether the site may require a stage 2 intrusive site assessment, to better define the nature and extent of potential contamination within the site; and • Provide advice on whether the site would be suitable (in the context of land contamination) for any proposed future rezoning.

Site Description

• The site covers an area of approximately 920 m2; • The site is currently zoned as E1: Local Centre; • The site is currently utilised within a commercial land-use setting; • The site is located within a mixed commercial-industrial and residential district.

Current aerial imagery suggests that the site is operating as ‘Audi Centre Mosman’. Imagery shows the site to contain one commercial warehouse structure and hardstand across the site surface.

Proposed Development

Reference is made to the draft Mosman Masterplan Structure Plan prepared by SJB Architecture, dated 29 July 2026 (Drawing No. 06, Revision 07). EGA understands that the site has been identified for increased development potential, however, have not been provided with any specific redevelopment plans. EGA has made the assumption that future development may include the demolition of current structures, rezoning of land, subdivision of land, and construction of new structures and infrastructure. EGA has not been made aware of any basement plans and have presumed that they are proposed as part of the future development.

Scope of Work

EGA undertook the following scope of works to address the project objectives:

• A desktop review of relevant information pertaining to the site; and • Assessment and reporting of the desktop review.

Investigation Outcome

Based on EGA’s assessment of the desktop review information, in the context of the proposed redevelopment scenario, EGA has made the following conclusions:

• The search of historical aerial imagery indicated a moderate potential for contamination within the site to have occurred, associated with uncontrolled demolition and potential site filling

August 2026

Attachment 1.1.10 Mosman Council

1162

Desktop Phase 1 Environmental Site Assessment 309 Military Road, Mosman NSW 3861-PSI-08-290726.v2f

Page 3

beneath slabs. EGA also notes that the building fabrics may contain hazardous building materials; • A review of publicly available contaminated land databases indicated that the site is not the subject of any licences, applications, notices under Section 308 of the Protection of the Environment Operations Act 1997. The site is not notified under Section 60 of the Contaminated Land Management Act 1997. The site is not the subject of any notifications under Section 58 of the Contaminated Land Management Act 1997; • The contaminants and areas of environmental concern (AEC), requiring further consideration or assessment, and possibly remediation or risk management, are the following: o AEC-1: Potential Chemical Storage – Leaks, Spills or Poor Management; o AEC-2: Wastewater Treatment and Drainage Infrastructure – Wash Bays, Traps, Pits and Pipes; o AEC-3: Potential Fuel Infrastructure; o AEC-4: Potential Uncontrolled Fill and Surface Soils; and o AEC-5: On-Site Structures - Hazardous Building Materials. • Soils not impacted by contamination may meet excavated natural material (ENM) or General Solid Waste (GSW) classification.

Conclusions and Recommendations

Notwithstanding the above, the potential contaminants of concern and affected environmental media are consistent with those typically encountered on brownfield redevelopment sites within the Sydney Basin. Any contamination that may be present is not expected to be of such an extent or complexity that it could not be effectively managed or remediated using conventional and readily available remediation techniques. Accordingly, the potential for contamination at the site is not considered to preclude proposed future redevelopment.

As detailed development plans are prepared for individual areas of the site, the existing environmental data should be reviewed in conjunction with the overall remedial strategy to confirm the nature and extent of any contamination relevant to the proposed works. Where required, additional assessment should be undertaken, and appropriate remediation and/or management measures developed, to demonstrate that the relevant portion of the site is, or can be made, suitable for its intended use.

This report, including its conclusions and recommendations, must be read in conjunction with the limitations presented in Section 12.

1. INTRODUCTION

Environmental Group Australia Pty Ltd (EGA) was engaged by Mosman Council (hereafter referred to as ‘the client’), to undertake a Desktop Phase 1 Environmental Site Assessment of the property located within Lot 1 DP936479, Lot 1 DP936480 and Lot 6 DP1099253, 309 Military Road, Mosman NSW (hereafter referred to as ‘the site’) (refer to Figure 1 and Figure 2).

The client is preparing to lodge a planning proposal to implement the draft Mosman Masterplan, which proposes increased housing capacity across the study area, including amendments to the planning controls at the site to facilitate redevelopment.

1.1.

Purpose and Objectives

A Desktop Phase 1 Environmental Site Assessment was required to assess the likelihood that the site had been contaminated by past or present activities and to form a preliminary view on the extent and degree of contamination. The objectives of the investigation were to:

• Provide advice on whether the site may require a stage 2 intrusive site assessment, to better define the nature and extent of potential contamination within the site; and • Provide advice on whether the site would be suitable (in the context of land contamination) for any proposed future rezoning.

1.2.

Site Description

• The site covers an area of approximately 920 m2; • The site is currently zoned as E1: Local Centre; • The site is currently utilised within a commercial land-use setting; • The site is located within a mixed commercial-industrial and residential district.

Current aerial imagery suggests that the site is operating as ‘Audi Centre Mosman’. Imagery shows the site to contain one commercial warehouse structure and hardstand across the site surface.

1.3.

Proposed Development

Reference is made to the draft Mosman Masterplan Structure Plan prepared by SJB Architecture, dated 29 July 2026 (Drawing No. 06, Revision 07). EGA understands that the site has been identified for increased development potential, however, have not been provided with any specific redevelopment plans. EGA has made the assumption that future development may include the demolition of current structures, rezoning of land, subdivision of land, and construction of new structures and infrastructure. EGA has not been made aware of any basement plans and have presumed that they are proposed as part of the future development.

1.4.

3. PHYSICAL SITE CONDITIONS

Regional geology, topography, soil landscape and hydrogeological information are presented in Table 3.1.

Table 3.1 Regional Setting Information Attribute Description

Geology

A review of the Environment NSW ‘eSpade V2.2’ web application (environment.nsw.gov.au/eSpade2WebApp, accessed 24 July 2026), indicated that the site is likely to be underlain by Hawkesbury Sandstone, which is a medium to coarse-grained quartz sandstone with minor shale and laminite lenses.

Soil Profiles

A review of the Environment NSW ‘eSpade V2.2’ web application (environment.nsw.gov.au/eSpade2WebApp, accessed 24 July 2026), indicated that the site is likely to contain: • gy1 (Topsoil): Loose, acidic, water-repellent coarse sandy loam, high permeability, low water capacity. • gy2 (Subsoil/Sandstone): Earthy, hard setting yellowish-brown clayey sand, low water capacity. • gy3 (Subsoil/Sandstone): Earthy/weakly pedal sandy clay loam, low permeability and wet strength. • gy4 (Subsoil/Shale): Highly pedal, highly erodible yellowish-brown clay, low permeability and wet strength. • Associated: Surface leaf litter (up to 10 cm) and loose quartz sand wash.

Terrain and Structure

A review of the Environment NSW ‘eSpade V2.2’ web application (environment.nsw.gov.au/eSpade2WebApp, accessed 24 July 2026), indicated that the site is likely to comprise: • Crests (<50 cm deep): Gradual transition of gy1 over bedrock, gy2, or occasionally gy3. Hard setting layers or bedrock expose when eroded. • Side slopes (30–70 cm deep): Discontinuous soils with up to 25% rock outcrop. gy1 overlies bedrock or gy2/gy3 on benches. • Shale Lenses (<100 cm deep): Sharp transition from gy1 to deep gy4 clay on inner benches. • Drainage Lines (<100 cm deep): Deep gy1 directly over bedrock.

Acid Sulfate Soils

A review of the NSW Government SEED web application (geo.seed.nsw.gov.au, accessed 24 July 2026), indicates that the site lies in an area mapped as ‘No Known Occurrence’ with respect to acid sulfate soils. This infers that land management activities are not likely to be affected by acid sulfate soils. Further assessment of acid sulfate soils in the context of this investigation is considered by EGA as not warranted.

Topography

Generally, the local landscape consists of undulating to rolling low hills with local relief 20–80 m and slopes of 10–25%. Sideslopes with narrow to wide outcropping sandstone rock benches (10–100 m), often forming broken scarps of <5 m. The site is considered generally flat, is located at an elevation approximately 87 m Australian Height Datum (AHD).

Hydrology and Hydrogeology

Surface water courses proximal to the site include Willoughby Creek located approximately 789 m to the northwest of the site, Quakers Hat Bay located approximately 1.41 km to the northeast and Mosman Bay located approximately 938 m to the south of the site. Based on the site topography and distances to the nearest surface water course, groundwater flow in the vicinity of the site is considered likely to be towards the northwest. A review of the NSW Office of Water groundwater database undertaken on 24 July 2026 indicated no registered groundwater features located within 500 m of the site.

Attribute Description

Adjacent Sensitive Receptors

The closest ecological receptors to the site include vegetated areas within the site / neighbouring properties and the closest receiving watercourses as outlined above. A review of the Bureau of Meteorology (BoM) Groundwater Dependent Ecosystem (GDE) Atlas Map was undertaken on 24 July 2026 to determine the closest sensitive ecological receptors. The closest sensitive receptor to the site included Manly Lagoon, located approximately 6.62 km to the northeast of the site and noted as a high potential groundwater dependent ecosystem. The closest sensitive human receptors are the residential properties surrounding the site’s boundary and any future onsite construction workers/builders.

Perfluoroalkyl Substances (PFAS)

A review of the publicly available NSW Government PFAS Investigation Program mapping information was reviewed 24 July 2026. The review was undertaken to identify any investigation localities that may affect the site. The site is not considered to be affected by any PFAS investigation programs.

Unexploded Ordnance (UXO)

A review of the publicly available Unexploded Ordnance (UXO) mapping information was reviewed 24 July 2026. The review was undertaken to identify any recorded UXO-related constraints or risks applicable to the site and its surrounding area at the time of reporting. The site is not considered to be affected by UXO.

The aerial imagery indicated a moderate potential for land contaminating activities to have occurred on the site including uncontrolled demolition and historical asbestos and pesticide application beneath slabs. Based on these possibilities, a Stage 2 Intrusive Assessment is recommended, should any works be proposed below the hardstand layer on-site. EGA also notes that on-site structures may contain hazardous building materials, and a hazardous building material survey (HBMS) is recommended prior to any demolition of on-site structures.

5. REGULATORY RECORDS

5.1.

NSW EPA CLM Act Register of Notified Sites and Notices

The search of the Public Register (the Contaminated Land Public Record) undertaken on 13 July 2026, indicated that the site was not the subject of any Notices recorded under Section 58 of the Contaminated Land Management Act 1997. The site has not been declared ‘significantly contaminated’.

A search of the list of contaminated sites notified to the EPA, did not identify the site as having been notified to the EPA under Section 60 of the CLM Act.

Six localities within the suburb of Mosman were also listed on the register, those being:

• 7-Eleven Mosman, 162A Spit Road Corner Mitchell Road (Service Station) – located approximately 1.6 km northeast of the site. No further regulation of this locality is required under the CLM Act; • BP Service Station & BP Express Mosman, 175 Ourimbah Road (Service Station) – located approximately 753 m north of the site. No further regulation of this locality is required under the CLM Act; • 7-Eleven Service Station Mosman, 45 Spit Road (Service Station) – located approximately 1.24 km northeast of the site. No further regulation of this locality is required under the CLM Act; • Allan Border Oval, Myahgah Road (Landfill) – located approximately 895 m east of the site. No further regulation of this locality is required under the CLM Act; and • 173 Avenue Road (Unclassified) – located approximately 1.16 km southeast of the site. No further regulation of this locality is required under the CLM Act.

As none of the localities are located within the immediate vicinity of the site, nor do they require management under the CLM Act, they are not considered to pose a contamination risk to the site.

A copy of the CLM Act Record of Notices search records is presented in Appendix D.

5.2.

NSW EPA POEO Act Register of Licences, Applications and Notices

A search of the publicly available online NSW EPA Record of Notices issued under Section 308 of the Protection of the Environment Operations Act 1997 was completed on 13 July 2026. The results indicated that the site was not the subject of any licences, applications, notices, audits or pollution studies or reduction programs.

A copy of the POEO Act Register of Licences, Applications and Notices search record is presented in Appendix D.

5.3.

Dangerous Goods Licences Search

A search of the register was not considered warranted due to the client scope for this report. A Dangerous Goods Licences Search is to be conducted if intrusive works are proposed for the site, due to the current site use as a petrol station.

7. CONCEPTUAL SITE MODEL

The conceptual site model (CSM) identifies and describes contaminants of concern, and the areas and mediums in which they are present (the AECs), which may cause a risk to human health or the environment. The CSM specifies the potential receptors of the risks and the exposure pathways by which the receptors may be impacted. The potential risks listed in the CSM are those that may exist prior to any contaminant remediation or management measures and are based on information gained from the preliminary site investigation (PSI).

7.1.

Potential Areas of Environmental Concern

The site history data collected, and site inspection observations, were assessed within the objectives of this investigation and in the context of the past and current land-use. The identified areas of environmental concern (AEC) and contaminants of concern (COC) which have the potential to be present on site are presented in Table 7.1 below.

Table 7.1 Areas of Environmental Concern (AEC) and Contaminants of Concern

AEC Areas of Environmental Concern and Contaminating Activity Resulting Risks Contaminants of Potential Concern

AEC-1 Potential Chemical Storage – Leaks, Spills or Poor Management

Leaching to shallow soils, vapour intrusion into future buildings and potential migration to groundwater.

Metals, TRH, BTEX, VOCs, OCPs, OPPs, Phenols and PFAS

AEC-2

Wastewater Treatment and Drainage Infrastructure – Wash Bays, Traps, Pits and Pipes

Structural failure or leakage resulting in soil and groundwater contamination and vapour intrusion.

Metals, TRH, BTEX, VOCs, MBAS (surfactants) and PFAS

AEC-3 Potential Fuel Infrastructure

Deep soil impact, LNAPL accumulation, groundwater impacts and vapour intrusion.

Metals, TRH, BTEX, VOCs, PAHs and Phenols

AEC-4 Potential Uncontrolled Fill and Surface Soils

Potential Chemical and Asbestos Contamination Above Human Health and Ecological Criteria

Metals, PAH, TRH, BTEX, OCP, OPP, PCBs and Asbestos

AEC-5 On-Site Structures - Hazardous Building Materials

Potential Human Health Exposure

Asbestos, lead in paint, SMF and PCBs

Notes to Table: TRH = Total Recoverable Hydrocarbons. BTEX = Benzene, Toluene, Ethylbenzene, Xylene. VOC = Volatile Organic Compounds. PAH = Polycyclic Aromatic Hydrocarbons. OCP = Organochlorine Pesticides. OPP = Organophosphorus Pesticides. PCB = Polychlorinated Biphenyls. SMF = Synthetic Mineral Fibres.

7.3.

Site Characteristics Affecting Risks

The following site characteristics, and exposure pathways, were taken into account when formulating the conceptual site model.

7.3.1. Land-Use Setting

EGA has not been advised on the future land-use. However, EGA understands that it is located within a mixed-use residential and commercial area.

As future redevelopment plans are currently unknown, EGA recommends conservatively adopting the ‘HIL A – Low-Density Residential’ land-use criteria per ASC NEPM (2013), for the purpose of assessing land contamination exposure risks within the site. A less conservative land-use setting may be adopted in future, subject to the specific details of any proposed development.

7.3.2. Surface Water

Surface water courses proximal to the site Willoughby Creek located approximately 789 m to the northwest of the site, Quakers Hat Bay located approximately 1.41 km to the northeast and Mosman Bay located approximately 938 m to the south of the site. These surface waters could be receptors for contaminants in site soils, from surface stormwater runoff and from other stormwater discharges.

August 2026

Attachment 1.1.10 Mosman Council

1175

Desktop Phase 1 Environmental Site Assessment 309 Military Road, Mosman NSW 3861-PSI-08-290726.v2f

Page 16

7.3.3. Groundwater – Drinking Water Risks

Currently, there are no groundwater bores on, or down-gradient of the site registered as domestic for potential drinking water use. The site is underlain by the Hawkesbury Sandstone, groundwater yield and quality are highly variable, making the likelihood that the groundwater would be extracted for drinking, low. It is also considered unlikely that domestic bores will be installed as part of a future redevelopment. Furthermore, the area is serviced by a town water supply, making human consumption of groundwater unlikely. Consequently, the exposure pathway for human consumption of groundwater remains incomplete. Historical commercial/industrial use of the site and its current use as ‘Audi Centre Mosman’ present the potential risk of mobile contaminants (such as TRH, BTEX and VOCs) leaching through soils into groundwater. Groundwater assessment with consideration to appropriate groundwater guidelines are to be considered.

7.3.4. Recreational Water Use and Aquatic Ecosystems

Future construction works involving any deep sub-grade disturbances, such as the excavation of underground basement parking levels may intersect groundwater. Should any excavation penetrate or sit immediately above the water table, it introduces the potential for groundwater inflow, dewatering requirements, or the accumulation of volatile vapours. A targeted groundwater assessment with consideration to Groundwater Investigation Levels (GIL), will be required prior to or during the design phase of any deep basement excavations, to confirm local water table depths and potential for contaminant migration. Groundwater within the site, if impacted by historical site activities, has the potential to discharge to nearby marine receiving waters. This poses a risk to aquatic ecosystems and recreational users of the water systems.

While the distances to the closest surface water body (> 700 m) provide potential for attenuation and dilution, aquatic ecosystem protection criteria (ASC NEPM 2013 / ANZG 2018) and recreational water guidelines will need to be considered.

7.3.5. Terrestrial Ecosystems

The proposed development may include gardens and other landscaping, and the impact of soil contaminants may affect plant well-being (plant growth and health). Ecological investigation levels (EILs) and ecological screening levels (ESLs) are to be considered based on the proposed future land- use.

7.3.6. Human Exposures to Site Contaminants

Due to the historical commercial/industrial uses of the site, volatile organic compounds (VOCs, TRH, BTEX, or MBAS) present the potential for contaminants within site soils and soil vapour. Future site occupants may be exposed to contaminants in soil – via dust inhalation, dermal contact and ingestion and if present, volatile contaminants can migrate into current and future building footprints or excavations.

Further consideration of human health exposure risks with consideration to ASC NEPM 2013 Human Investigation Levels (HIL) and Human Screening Levels (HSL) criteria is considered warranted. The potential for volatile contaminants warrants field screening with a Photoionisation Detector, during any future soil investigations.

9. PLANNING AND MASTER PLAN ADVICE

9.1.

Suitability for Future Development

In accordance with Section 4.6 of State Environmental Planning Policy (Resilience and Hazards) 2021, a planning authority must consider land contamination risks when determining a Development Application (DA) or rezoning proposal.

From a planning perspective, the historical operation of the site as ‘Audi Centre Mosman’ presents potential contamination risks but does not preclude any future redevelopment plans. Because intrusive sampling has not yet been conducted, the site cannot currently be classified as suitable for any works, in its present state. Intrusive characterisation is required via a Stage 2 Environmental Site Assessment (ESA) to determine if any contamination is present on-site and determine if management or remediation is required.

9.2.

Identification of Potential Issues

Major potential issues and constraints are outlined below and must be considered in early-stage planning.

If future development includes sub-grade structures (e.g., basement parking, underground storage, or deep footings), vapour intrusion risks from potential volatile organic compounds or chlorinated solvents must be evaluated. Any designs intersecting or sitting immediately above the water table may require vapour mitigation barriers, active or passive venting, or tanking systems.

If excavation works intersect groundwater, dewatering will require chemical characterisation prior to discharge into stormwater (subject to Council approval) or sewer (under a Sydney Water trade waste agreement).

Soil excavated for basement parking, footings, or trenching will require formal waste classification in accordance with the NSW EPA Waste Classification Guidelines (2014), prior to off-site disposal. Natural excavated materials may qualify as Virgin Excavated Natural Material (VENM) or Excavated Natural Material (ENM), while uncontrolled fill or historical soils may be classified as General Solid Waste (GSW), Restricted Solid Waste, or Hazardous Waste, carrying significant cost implications.

9.3.

Requirements for the Development Application (DA) Stage

To satisfy regulatory requirements for a change of land-use, the following works are required to support a future DA:

• Environmental Site Assessment (ESA) - Implementation of a systematic intrusive soil, soil vapour, and groundwater sampling carried out in accordance with guidelines made or approved under Section 105 of the Contaminated Land Management Act 1997 (CLM Act); and • Remediation Action Plan (RAP): If the ESA identifies contamination exceeding the criteria relevant to the proposed land-use, a RAP must be prepared to detail how the site will be safely remediated or managed. Following remediation, a Validation Report must be completed to verify site suitability.

9.4.

Suggestion Inclusion to Master Planning

EGA recommends the following condition appropriate for inclusion in planning documentation relating to site redevelopment or rezoning:

"Prior to the determination of any Development Application (DA) proposing land-use change, ground disturbance, or sub-surface excavation, a Stage 2 Environmental Site Assessment (ESA) must be undertaken in accordance with guidelines made or approved by the NSW EPA under Section 105 of the Contaminated Land Management Act 1997.

CONTAMINANTS OF CONCERN

The contaminants of concern, determined from the considerations of the site’s history and from observations during a site inspection, are the following:

• Metals - arsenic, cadmium, chromium, copper, lead, nickel, zinc and mercury; • PAHs - polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons; • BTEX - benzene, toluene, ethylbenzene, xylene; • TRH - total recoverable hydrocarbons; • OPPs - organophosphorus pesticides; • OCPs - organochlorine pesticides; • PCBs - polychlorinated biphenyls; • VOCs - volatile organic compounds (petroleum and chlorinated); • MBAS (Surfactants) - methylene blue active substances; • PFAS - per- and polyfluoroalkyl substances; • Phenols; and • Asbestos - fines/friable (AF/FA) and bonded asbestos containing materials (ACM).

It should be noted that although the above list of compounds and contaminant groups constitutes the contaminants of concern, as identified in this preliminary site investigation, additional contaminants may also be analysed during any planned intrusive site sampling investigation as those contaminants may be obtained from the same analytical processes and may aid the overall assessment of the site’s condition.

CONCLUSIONS AND RECOMMENDATIONS

11.1. Site Condition and Further Requirements

Based on EGA’s assessment of the desktop review information, in the context of the proposed redevelopment scenario, EGA has made the following conclusions:

• The search of historical aerial imagery indicated a moderate potential for contamination within the site to have occurred, associated with uncontrolled demolition and potential site filling beneath slabs. EGA also notes that the building fabrics may contain hazardous building materials; • A review of publicly available contaminated land databases indicated that the site is not the subject of any licences, applications, notices under Section 308 of the Protection of the Environment Operations Act 1997. The site is not notified under Section 60 of the Contaminated Land Management Act 1997. The site is not the subject of any notifications under Section 58 of the Contaminated Land Management Act 1997; • The contaminants and areas of environmental concern (AEC), requiring further consideration or assessment, and possibly remediation or risk management, are the following: o AEC-1: Potential Chemical Storage – Leaks, Spills or Poor Management; o AEC-2: Wastewater Treatment and Drainage Infrastructure – Wash Bays, Traps, Pits and Pipes; o AEC-3: Potential Fuel Infrastructure; o AEC-4: Potential Uncontrolled Fill and Surface Soils; and o AEC-5: On-Site Structures - Hazardous Building Materials. • Soils not impacted by contamination may meet excavated natural material (ENM) or General Solid Waste (GSW) classification.

11.2. Recommendations

are consistent with those typically encountered on brownfield redevelopment sites within the Sydney Basin. Any contamination that may be present is not expected to be of such an extent or complexity that it could not be effectively managed or remediated using conventional and readily available remediation techniques. Accordingly, the potential for contamination at the site is not considered to preclude proposed future redevelopment.

As detailed development plans are prepared for individual areas of the site, the existing environmental data should be reviewed in conjunction with the overall remedial strategy to confirm the nature and extent of any contamination relevant to the proposed works. Where required, additional assessment should be undertaken, and appropriate remediation and/or management measures developed, to demonstrate that the relevant portion of the site is, or can be made, suitable for its intended use.

APPENDIX D

NSW EPA PUBLIC REGISTER

August 2026

Attachment 1.1.10 Mosman Council

1195

Background

A strategy to systematically prioritise, assess and respond to notifications under Section 60 of the Contaminated Land Management Act 1997 (CLM Act) has been developed by the EPA. This strategy acknowledges the EPA’s obligations to make information available to the public under Government Information (Public Access) Act 2009 .

When a site is notified to the EPA, it may be accompanied by detailed site reports where the owner has been proactive in addressing the contamination and its source. However, often there is minimal information on the nature or extent of the contamination.

After receiving a report, the first step is to confirm that the report does not relate to a pollution incident. The Protection of the Environment Operations Act 1997 (POEO Act) deals with pollution incidents, waste stockpiling or dumping. The EPA also has an incident management process to manage significant incidents (https://www.epa.nsw.gov.au/reporting-and-incidents/incident- management).

In many cases, the information indicates the contamination is securely immobilised within the site, such as under a building or carpark, and is not currently causing any significant risks for the community or environment. Such sites may still need to be cleaned up, but this can be done in conjunction with any subsequent building or redevelopment of the land. These sites do not require intervention under the CLM Act, and are dealt with through the planning and development consent process. In these cases, the EPA informs the local council or other planning authority, so that the information can be recorded and considered at the appropriate time (https://www.epa.nsw.gov.au/your-environment/contaminated-land/managing-contaminated-land/role-of-planning-authorities).

Where indications are that the contamination could cause actual harm to the environment or an unacceptable offsite impact (i.e. the land is 'significantly contaminated'), the EPA would apply the regulatory provisions of the CLM Act to have the responsible polluter and/or landowner investigate and remediate the site. If the reported contamination could present an immediate or long-term threat to human health NSW Health will be consulted. SafeWork NSW and Water NSW can also be consulted if there appear to be occupational health and safety risks or an impact on groundwater quality.

As such, the sites notified to the EPA and presented in the list of contaminated sites notified to the EPA are at various stages of the assessment and remediation process. Understanding the nature of the underlying contamination, its implications and implementing a remediation program where required, can take a considerable period of time. The list provides an indication, in relation to each nominated site, as to the management status of that particular site. Further detailed information may be available from the EPA or the person who notified the site.

The following questions and answers may assist those interested in this issue.

Frequently asked questions

Why does my land appear on the list of notified sites?

Your land may appear on the list because:

• the site owner and/or the polluter has notified the EPA under section 60 of the CLM Act • the EPA has been notified via other means and is satisfied that the site is or was contaminated.

If a site is on the list, it does not necessarily mean the contamination is significant enough to regulate under the CLM Act.

List current as at 9 April 2026 1 of 139

August 2026

Attachment 1.1.10 Mosman Council

1196

Does the list contain all contaminated sites in NSW?

No. The list only contains contaminated sites that EPA is aware of. If a site is not on the list, it does not necessarily mean the site is not contaminated.

The EPA relies on responsible parties and the public to notify contaminated sites.

How are notified contaminated sites managed by the EPA?

There are different ways the EPA can manage notified contaminated sites. Options include:

• regulation under the CLM Act, POEO Act, or both • notifying the relevant planning authority for management under the planning and development process • managing the site under the Protection of the Environment Operation (Underground Petroleum Storage Systems) Regulation 2014.

There are specific cases where contamination is managed under a tailored program operated by another agency (for example, the Resources & Geoscience's Legacy Mines Program).

What should I do if I am a potential buyer of a site that appears on the list?

You should seek advice from the seller to understand the contamination issue. You may need to seek independent contamination or legal advice.

The information provided in the list is indicative only and a starting point for your own assessment. Land contamination from past site uses is common, mainly in urban environments. If the site is properly remediated or managed, it may not affect the intended future use of the site.

Who can I contact if I need more information about a site?

You can contact the Environment Line at any time by calling 131 555 or by emailing info@environment.nsw.gov.au.

You should obtain independent advice before you make any decision based on the information in the list.

The list is made available on the understanding that the EPA, its servants and agents, to the extent permitted by law, accept no responsibility for any damage, cost, loss or expense incurred by you as a result of:

1.            any information in the list; or 2.            any error, omission or misrepresentation in the list; or 3.            any malfunction or failure to function of the list; 4.            without limiting (2) or (3) above, any delay, failure or error in recording, displaying or updating information.

The EPA may be regulating contamination at the site through a licence or notice under the Protection of the Environment Operations Act 1997 (POEO Act). Contamination at the site may be being managed under the planning process.
